I know I keep circling around the same answer, but no, it’s not after almost all of them.\n\nIt’s more like there are certain times when it gets really intense — like if I come home and I’ve eaten way too much takeout, or I start with snacks and then it turns into a lot more than I meant. Then I get this feeling like it’s just sitting in me, like I can almost feel it there, and my mind goes straight to, “This is going to turn into weight if I don’t get it out.” That’s when I’ll go to the bathroom and make myself throw up.\n\nBut it doesn’t happen after every out-of-control eating episode, or even almost all of them. It’s more of a sometimes thing, when the panic about it being in me gets bad enough.\n\nDoctor: Is making yourself throw up something that you do after more than half of your out-of-control eating episodes?\n\nPatient: No, I don’t think it’s more than half. I mean, it happens, and it feels important when it does, but it’s not every time or even most times.\n\nIt’s usually after those times when I get home and I’ve eaten too much takeout, or I start snacking and it just keeps going, and then I get this really gross, panicky feeling like the food is just sitting in me. My mind goes to, “If I leave it there, it’s going to turn into weight,” and then I’ll go to the bathroom and make myself throw up to feel like I’ve undone it somehow.\n\nBut if you’re asking more than half of the out-of-control eating episodes, I’d say no. Less than half, probably. It’s more like when the panic really spikes.\n\nDoctor: Do you make yourself throw up after less than half of your out-of-control eating episodes?\n\nPatient: Yes… less than half. I mean, it’s not like every time I feel out of control with eating, I automatically go and do that. It’s more… certain times, when it feels really bad afterward.\n\nLike if I get home and I’ve had too much takeout, or I start eating snacks and it kind of snowballs, then I’ll get this really panicky feeling that it’s just sitting in me. And I start thinking, almost obsessively, that if it stays there it’s going to turn into weight. That’s when I’ll go to the bathroom and make myself throw up, because in that moment it feels like I have to get it out.\n\nBut if you’re asking less than half, yes, I’d say that’s right. It happens sometimes, but not most of the time.\n\nDoctor: Is purging something you do only after binge eating episodes?\n\nPatient: Yeah… pretty much only after those out-of-control eating times. It’s not like I just throw up after a normal meal or something.\n\nIt’s usually when I get home and I’ve eaten too much takeout, or I start with snacks and then it turns into way more than I meant to eat. Then I get this feeling like it’s sitting in me, and I start panicking that it’s going to turn into weight if I don’t get it out. That’s when I’ll go to the bathroom and make myself throw up.\n\nSo, yes, I’d say it’s tied to those binge-type episodes, not something I do randomly after regular eating.\n\nDoctor: Do you purge at least once a week?\n\nPatient: Yeah… I think it is at least once a week sometimes. It kind of depends on the week, but there are definitely weeks where it happens.\n\nIt’s usually after I get home and I’ve eaten too much takeout, or I start picking at snacks and then it turns into way more than I meant to eat. Since my apartment move kind of settled down and my routine became more predictable, it’s usually at least once a week that I’ll have one of those late-night episodes.\n\nIt tends to happen at night, after work or after I’ve been home for a while, when I’m by myself. I’ll start out thinking I’m just having something small or finishing dinner, and then it turns into eating way past what I meant to. Not every single night or anything like that, but most weeks there’s at least one night where it happens.\n\nAnd then afterward I usually try to make up for it somehow, like throwing up, or telling myself I’m just going to skip breakfast the next day, or forcing myself to go on a really long run even if I don’t actually feel up to it. So… yeah, I guess if I’m being honest, about weekly, sometimes more, but usually at least once in a week.\n\nDoctor: You mentioned trying to make up for these episodes, can you tell me more about what you mean by that?\n\nPatient: Yeah… so, when I say “make up for it,” I mean I’ll try to sort of cancel it out the next day. Like if I had a bad night where I ate a lot and felt out of control, I wake up already thinking I need to do something about it.\n\nA lot of times that means I’ll go run extra miles on the trail in the morning, even if I’m tired or my legs feel heavy or I didn’t sleep well. I’ll tell myself, “Just go farther today,” or “You have to burn some of that off.” It doesn’t really feel like a normal workout then. It feels more like a punishment, I guess, or like I’m trying to undo what happened the night before.\n\nOther times I’ll skip breakfast before my shift. I’ll be getting ready for work and even if I’m hungry, I’ll think, “No, you don’t need breakfast after what you ate last night.” And then I’ll just have coffee or water and go in. I know that probably sounds stupid, because it doesn’t actually erase anything, but in the moment it feels like I have to do something or I’ll just sit with that gross, guilty feeling all day.\n\nIt’s not always some big planned thing. It’s more this pressure in my head, like I owe a debt from the night before and I need to pay it back somehow.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever made yourself throw up after one of these episodes of eating?\n\nPatient: Yeah… I have. I don’t really like saying that, but yes.\n\nIt’s not every single time, but sometimes after I get home and I’ve eaten too much takeout or a bunch of snacks, I’ll end up going into the bathroom and making myself throw up. Usually it’s after I already feel like I went way past the point where I should’ve stopped, and then I start panicking about it just… sitting in me, I guess. Like if I don’t do something, it’s going to turn into weight.\n\nI know that probably sounds irrational, and I’m embarrassed saying it out loud. In the moment it feels like this urgent thing, like I have to get it out of me so I can stop thinking about it. Then afterward I don’t exactly feel good, it’s more like relief mixed with feeling pretty ashamed that I did it again.\n\nDoctor: How often do you make yourself throw up after one of these episodes of eating?\n\nPatient: It’s… not every time. I want to be clear about that, because sometimes I’ll just sit with the awful feeling afterward, or I’ll tell myself I won’t eat the next morning, but there are times where I do make myself throw up.\n\nI’d say it happens sometimes after those bigger episodes, especially when I’ve gotten home and eaten too much takeout or a bunch of snacks and then I start feeling panicky, like it’s just sitting in me. That’s usually when I’ll go into the bathroom and do it. It feels like, if I don’t get it out, it’s going to turn into weight, even though I know that probably sounds… not logical.\n\nSo, frequency-wise, I don’t know the exact number. It’s not daily, and it’s not after every episode, but it does happen sometimes when I feel like I’ve really gone overboard.\n\nDoctor: Have you noticed any significant changes in your weight over time due to these episodes and your attempts to make up for them?\n\nPatient: I’m honestly not totally sure. I mean, I notice my weight, probably more than I should, but I don’t know that I can say there’s been some huge, clear change over time because of it.\n\nIt kind of feels like it goes up and down a little, and I’ll get really focused on those small changes, like if my clothes feel tighter one day or the number is different, I’ll read way too much into it. But I don’t think I’ve had some dramatic weight loss or gain that I could point to and say, “That’s from the eating episodes and then trying to make up for them.”\n\nIt’s more that I’m constantly worried it’s going to change, if that makes sense. Like the fear of gaining weight is there, and that drives a lot of what I do afterward, but as far as actual significant changes… I don’t know. I haven’t noticed anything major, or at least nothing I’m confident saying is significant.\n\nDoctor: Is the fear of gaining weight what motivates you to try to make up for these episodes of eating by restricting your food intake or purging?\n\nPatient: Yeah… I think that’s a big part of it. I don’t like admitting that, because it sounds so shallow when I say it out loud, but honestly, yes. It’s the fear of gaining weight, or even just looking like I gained weight.\n\nLike, I work around people all day at the venue, and I’m visible. Guests see me, coworkers see me, and even when people have told me I look too lean or that I don’t need to worry, I still don’t really believe it. If my pants feel even a little tighter, or if the scale is up by like a pound, I get this immediate panic feeling. It’s like my brain jumps straight to, “Everyone’s going to notice. They’re going to think I let myself go.”\n\nAnd I know one pound probably isn’t something anyone else can see. I can say that logically. But in the moment it feels very real, like I have to do something to undo it before it shows. So yeah, that fear is what pushes me to try to make up for it, whether that’s not eating as much afterward or purging. It’s not really about feeling healthy or anything like that. It’s more like I’m trying to stop that panic and stop myself from feeling like I’m suddenly different or bigger.\n\nDoctor: How many times a month would you say you make yourself throw up after one of these episodes of eating?\n\nPatient: I’d say… maybe a few times a month? It’s hard to give an exact number because I don’t really track it, and honestly I try not to think of it that way. But if I’m estimating, it’s probably around two or three times in a month, maybe a little more in a bad month.\n\nIt’s usually after I get home and I’ve eaten too much takeout or snacks, like I’ll keep picking at things and then suddenly it feels like I crossed some line. Then I get this panicky feeling, like it’s just sitting in me and it’s going to turn into weight if I don’t do something. That’s when I’ll go to the bathroom and make myself throw up.\n\nSo… not every week like clockwork, and not after every time I overeat, but sometimes. A few times a month is probably the closest answer.\n\nDoctor: On average, how many times a week would you say you have one of these episodes of eating where you feel out of control?\n\nPatient: On average… I think it’s usually about once a week. Not every single week in the exact same way, but since my apartment move kind of settled down and I got into a routine again, most weeks there’s at least one night where it happens.\n\nIt’s usually late at night, after work or after I’ve been trying to be “good” all day, and then I’ll start eating something small and it just turns into… more than I meant. Like way past what I planned, and I feel like I can’t really stop in the moment. Then afterward I get really upset with myself and I start trying to fix it somehow, like making myself throw up, or telling myself I’m not allowed to have breakfast the next day, or forcing myself to go on a long run even if I’m tired.\n\nSo if you’re asking for a number, I’d say maybe once a week on average. Sometimes more if it’s a rough week, but most weeks it’s at least one late-night episode like that.\n\nDoctor: Do you make yourself throw up after every episode of eating where you feel out of control?\n\nPatient: No, not every time. It’s… it’s more like sometimes, when it feels really bad afterward.\n\nLike if I get home and I’ve eaten too much takeout or I keep going back for snacks, and then I suddenly realize how much I had, that’s when I start feeling panicky. It feels like it’s just sitting in me, and I get this thought that if I don’t do something right away, it’s going to turn into weight or I’m going to wake up looking different. I know that probably sounds dramatic, but in the moment it feels urgent.\n\nSo there are times I’ll go to the bathroom and make myself throw up. But it’s not after every out-of-control eating episode. Sometimes I just sit with it and feel awful about it, or I try to tell myself I won’t do it again. But yeah, there are definitely times when I do make myself throw up afterward because I can’t stand the feeling of it being in me.\n\nDoctor: Do you make yourself throw up after most episodes of eating where you feel out of control, or is it less often than that?\n\nPatient: It’s less often than that. I don’t think it’s most of the time, no.\n\nIt’s more like… sometimes, after I get home and I’ve eaten too much takeout or I’ve been snacking and it turns into more than I meant, I get this really uncomfortable feeling that it’s just sitting in me. And then I start thinking about it turning into weight, like if I don’t do something right away it’s going to stay there or show up on me somehow. That’s usually when I’ll go to the bathroom and make myself throw up.\n\nBut it’s not every time I feel out of control eating, and I wouldn’t say it’s most episodes. It’s just… when the panic is bad enough, I guess.\n\nDoctor: Do you make yourself throw up more often than you restrict your food intake after one of these episodes of eating?\n\nPatient: I think the throwing up is the thing I do more… or at least it’s the one I’m more aware of right after it happens.\n\nLike, if I get home and I’ve had too much takeout or I keep eating snacks and it goes further than I meant it to, the thought that hits me is that it’s just sitting there in my stomach. And then I get really uncomfortable and panicky, like it’s going to turn into weight if I don’t do something. So sometimes I’ll go to the bathroom and make myself throw up, just to feel like it’s not in me anymore.\n\nRestricting afterward happens too in my head, but the vomiting is more of an immediate “I need to fix this right now” kind of thing. So yeah, I’d say I make myself throw up more often than I restrict after those episodes.\n\nDoctor: Is making yourself throw up something that happens after almost all of your out-of-control eating episodes, or is it less frequent than that?\n\nPatient: It’s less frequent than that. I know I keep circling around the same answer, but no, it’s not after almost all of them.\n\nIt’s more like there are certain times when it gets really intense — like if I come home and I’ve eaten way too much takeout, or I start with snacks and then it turns into a lot more than I meant. Then I get this feeling like it’s just sitting in me, like I can almost feel it there, and my mind goes straight to, “This is going to turn into weight if I don’t get it out.” That’s when I’ll go to the bathroom and make myself throw up.\n\nBut it doesn’t happen after every out-of-control eating episode, or even almost all of them. It’s more of a sometimes thing, when the panic about it being in me gets bad enough.\n\nDoctor: Is making yourself throw up something that you do after more than half of your out-of-control eating episodes?\n\nPatient: No, I don’t think it’s more than half. I mean, it happens, and it feels important when it does, but it’s not every time or even most times.\n\nIt’s usually after those times when I get home and I’ve eaten too much takeout, or I start snacking and it just keeps going, and then I get this really gross, panicky feeling like the food is just sitting in me. My mind goes to, “If I leave it there, it’s going to turn into weight,” and then I’ll go to the bathroom and make myself throw up to feel like I’ve undone it somehow.\n\nBut if you’re asking more than half of the out-of-control eating episodes, I’d say no. Less than half, probably. It’s more like when the panic really spikes.\n\nDoctor: Do you make yourself throw up after less than half of your out-of-control eating episodes?\n\nPatient: Yes… less than half. I mean, it’s not like every time I feel out of control with eating, I automatically go and do that. It’s more… certain times, when it feels really bad afterward.\n\nLike if I get home and I’ve had too much takeout, or I start eating snacks and it kind of snowballs, then I’ll get this really panicky feeling that it’s just sitting in me. And I start thinking, almost obsessively, that if it stays there it’s going to turn into weight. That’s when I’ll go to the bathroom and make myself throw up, because in that moment it feels like I have to get it out.\n\nBut if you’re asking less than half, yes, I’d say that’s right. It happens sometimes, but not most of the time.\n\nDoctor: Is purging something you do only after binge eating episodes?\n\nPatient: Yeah… pretty much only after those out-of-control eating times. It’s not like I just throw up after a normal meal or something.\n\nIt’s usually when I get home and I’ve eaten too much takeout, or I start with snacks and then it turns into way more than I meant to eat. Then I get this feeling like it’s sitting in me, and I start panicking that it’s going to turn into weight if I don’t get it out. That’s when I’ll go to the bathroom and make myself throw up.\n\nSo, yes, I’d say it’s tied to those binge-type episodes, not something I do randomly after regular eating.\n\nDoctor: Do you purge at least once a week?\n\nPatient: Yeah… I think it is at least once a week sometimes. It kind of depends on the week, but there are definitely weeks where it happens.\n\nIt’s usually after I get home and I’ve eaten too much takeout, or I start picking at snacks and then it turns into way more than I meant to eat. Then I get this really uncomfortable feeling, like the food is just sitting in me, and I start panicking that it’s going to turn into weight. That’s when I’ll go to the bathroom and make myself throw up.\n\nSo… yes, I’d say it can be once a week, maybe more in a bad week, but it’s tied to those specific times where I feel like I overdid it and can’t stand having it in me."
